(HD209458b; \S\ref{sec:lowerprofile}): the lower atmosphere handed over as a
table over an interval of pressure instead of the scalars of
\texttt{base.inp}. The base state ($T_0$, $R_0$, $p_{\rm base}$,
$q_{\rm H_2}$), the elemental reservoirs (He/H and solar C/N/O) and
$K_{zz}(r)$ all come from the one file; \texttt{make\_example\_profile.py}
regenerates it & \texttt{14\_diffusion} $+$ \texttt{Lower atmosphere
profile: lower\_atmosphere\_profile.dat}, \emph{minus} \texttt{He\_Kzz}
(the profile carries $K_{zz}$) and \emph{minus} \texttt{metals.inp}
(the profile carries C/N/O) \\
\texttt{18\_oxygen\_chemistry} & oxygen chemistry (HD209458b;
  \S\ref{sec:oxychem}): OH, H$_2$O and CO in the coupled system with the
  molecular network, the FUV photolysis of H$_2$O and OH, and the molecular
  carriers transported (the default with the oxygen cycle), so the base
  H$_2$/H partition is computed rather than imported. HD\,209458\,b's hot base
  is not the regime the option exists for (there collisional dissociation
  sets the partition); it is the example because it is a molecular
  configuration that converges & \texttt{16\_molecular\_metals} $+$
  \texttt{Oxygen chemistry: True}, \texttt{Stellar FUV B3/B4 flux},
  \texttt{Stellar Lya flux}, \texttt{Stellar LW flux: 480.9} \\
\texttt{19\_molecular\_ir\_bands} & molecular infrared bands (HD209458b):
  \texttt{18} plus the H$_2$ quadrupole and magnetic-dipole lines and the
  H$_2$O and CO vibration--rotation bands, each the net exchange with the
  diluted $B_\nu(T_0)$ that \texttt{Base IR field} supplies; writes the
  \texttt{H2\_IR}, \texttt{H2O\_IR} and \texttt{CO\_IR} columns of
  \texttt{Cooling\_breakdown.txt} & \texttt{18} $+$ \texttt{Base IR field:
  True}, \texttt{Molecular IR bands: True} \\
\end{longtable}}

% ===================================================================== %
\section{Comparison studies}\label{sec:compare}
% ===================================================================== %

\subsection{Effect of metal-line cooling (metals on vs.\ off)}
Trace metals add strong line cooling (and some photoionization heating and free
electrons). Figure~\ref{fig:metals} compares the tutorial planet with and without
\texttt{metals.inp}: metal-line cooling lowers the peak temperature by
$\sim1800$\,K (acting as a thermostat that prevents the H/He-only over-heating),
while the H ionization structure is barely changed. The effect is far stronger
for hotter, more irradiated planets (e.g.\ WASP-121b, where metal cooling is
essential to match the observed temperatures); the detailed cooling by channel
budget is in Figure~\ref{fig:cool}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/metals_on_off.pdf}
\caption{Metals on vs.\ off for the tutorial planet: metal-line cooling lowers
the peak temperature (left) while the H ionization fraction is nearly unchanged
(right).}\label{fig:metals}
\end{figure}

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.72\textwidth]{figures/cooling_breakdown.pdf}
\caption{Radiative cooling by channel vs.\ radius (WASP-121b, metals on; from
\texttt{Cooling\_breakdown.txt}). Below $\sim1.4\,R_p$ the metal lines (e.g.\
Fe\,II, Mg\,II, C/O) compete with or dominate the H/He collisional channels.}
\label{fig:cool}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Spherical vs.\ Roche (RLOF) and the mass-loss rate}
Removing the \texttt{Domain mode: Spherical} line switches on the full Roche
tidal potential, truncated at the L1 radius. Figure~\ref{fig:roche} compares a
spherical model (Case~A) and a Roche model (Case~B) of WASP-121b: the tidal
potential lowers the effective gravity toward the star, so the Roche wind
accelerates far faster and the mass-loss rate rises by an order of magnitude
($\dot M$ from $\sim0.05$ to $\sim0.34\,M_p$\,Gyr$^{-1}$ here), the
``Roche-lobe overflow'' enhancement.

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.98\textwidth]{figures/spherical_vs_roche.pdf}
\caption{Spherical (Case~A) vs.\ Roche (Case~B) WASP-121b: temperature,
velocity, and density. The Roche wind reaches L1 ($\approx1.6\,R_p$) and
accelerates much faster; $\dot M$ is annotated. The temperature panel is the
\texttt{\_adv} profile; its base is smooth because the post-processor falls back
to the equilibrium solution where the flow is not a clean outflow (see the caveat
in \S\ref{sec:outputs}). The Roche velocity (middle) dips slightly negative near
the base: the genuine, physical ``breathing'' inflow of a near-Roche-filling
planet.}\label{fig:roche}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Transmission spectra}
\texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} turns a converged model into transit absorption. The metal
resonance lines are the key NUV/optical diagnostics; Figure~\ref{fig:trans}
compares the modeled effective transit radii $R_{\rm eff}/R_\star$ (spherical
Case~A) against Huang et al.\ (2023). Recall the conventions of \S5.2: Mg\,II is a
4\,\AA\ bin, Ca\,II/Na are line-center, and $R_{\rm eff}/R_\star=\sqrt{(R_p/R_\star)^2+h}$.

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.66\textwidth]{figures/transmission_metals.pdf}
\caption{Metal-line transit radii $R_{\rm eff}/R_\star$ for spherical Case~A,
compared with Huang et al.\ (2023) Case~A and Case~D.}\label{fig:trans}
\end{figure}

% ===================================================================== %
\section{Atomic and molecular data}\label{sec:data}
% ===================================================================== %
By default EXHALE is an \emph{atomic/ionic} code: the modeled species are H, He
(including the He\,$2\,^3$S metastable triplet), and ten trace metals
(C, N, O, Mg, Si, Ca, Na, K, S, Fe) across their first two--three ionization
stages. The optional molecular network (\texttt{Molecular chemistry: True},
\S\ref{sec:loweratm}) adds H$_2$, H$_2^+$, H$_3^+$ and HeH$^+$ to the coupled
equilibrium, with H$_2$ photoionization opacity (Yan et al.\ 1998) and H$_3^+$
infrared cooling (Miller et al.\ 2013); \texttt{Oxygen chemistry: True} adds
OH, H$_2$O and CO (\S\ref{sec:oxychem}), and \texttt{Molecular IR bands: True}
the H$_2$ line spectrum (Roueff et al.\ 2019) and the H$_2$O and CO bands
(HITEMP) as infrared coolants. There is no H$^-$, and the only molecular
opacities are those of H$_2$ (its ionizing continuum and the Lyman--Werner
lines) and, with the oxygen chemistry, the FUV photodissociation of H$_2$O,
OH and CO.
Table~\ref{tab:data} collects the provenance of the atomic rates and cross
sections, grouped by physical process. Of the H/He data of the original ATES
(Caldiroli et al.\ 2021, 2022) the code keeps the hydrogenic cross sections
of H\,I and He\,II and, as comparison options, the legacy fits
(\texttt{ATES\_photoionization\_rate}, \texttt{Legacy\_HHe\_rates}); the
default recombination, collisional and cooling coefficients of H and He were
replaced after the atomic-data audits of 2026-09-24 to 09-26
(\path{md/Update_EXHALE_stage3.md} sections 17, 19 and 22), and the metal,
excited-hydrogen, and charge-exchange data were added in this work.

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash\hyphenpenalty=10000\exhyphenpenalty=10000}p{0.205\textwidth}p{0.30\textwidth}p{0.43\textwidth}@{}}

\texttt{excited\_hydrogen.f90}; \texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py} (transmission line
data).}\label{tab:data}\\

\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Photoionization cross sections}}\\[2pt]
Bound--free $\sigma(E)$ & H\,I, He\,II & hydrogenic (Kramers) analytic form on the measured thresholds (orig.\ ATES) \\
 & He\,I & Verner, Ferland, Korista \& Yakovlev (1996) default; legacy ATES 2-term fit via \texttt{ATES\_photoionization\_rate} \\

 & K\,I & Verner \& Yakovlev (1995) at every energy, outer shell and subshells \\

A neutral of C, N, O, Mg, Si, Ca, Fe that loses two or more electrons goes to its third stage in the balance; a jump beyond the carried stages is counted in the top carried stage \\
Opacity model (runtime) & all (via \texttt{opacity.inp}) & A=analytic above (default); C=constant above threshold, $\sigma =$ \texttt{OPA\_CONST\_}$X$ times the analytic value at the edge; P=that constant base times the pressure-broadening factor (Robinson \& Catling); T=tabulated files \\

Rate $C(T)$ & H\,I, He\,I, He\,II & Voronov (1997, ADNDT 65, 1) Table~I default; legacy ATES fits (Abel+1997 / Hui \& Gnedin 1997) via \texttt{Legacy\_HHe\_rates} \\
 & C--Fe, all stages & Voronov (1997) Table~I, each row with Voronov's own fitted $\Delta E$; the fits are stated for 1\,eV--20\,keV (3\,eV He\,II, 2\,eV N\,II, O\,II) and are evaluated below that range with their exact $e^{-U}$ threshold factor and no accuracy claim \\
 & He\,$2\,^3$S & Black (1981, MNRAS 197, 553) eq.~12, $8.38\times10^{-10}T^{1/2}e^{-55338/T}$ \\

\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Recombination (radiative RR + dielectronic DR)}}\\[2pt]
Ground capture $\alpha_1$ & H\,I, He\,I, He\,II & the Milne relation on the ground-state photoionization cross section the transfer uses, so the capture and the absorption of its photon are one process (0.998 of Hummer 1994 for H\,I, 1.006--1.011 of Hummer \& Storey 1998 for He\,I, as the code comment records them) \\
Case~B & H\,II, He\,II, He\,III & Badnell (2023) RR ($+$ Badnell DR for He\,II) minus that $\alpha_1$ default; legacy Hui \& Gnedin (1997) fits via \texttt{Legacy\_HHe\_rates}; Koskinen et al.\ (2022) Table~1 R1, R2 via \texttt{Atomic rate set} \\
Case~A where thin (def.\ on, \texttt{H\_rec\_escape}, \texttt{He\_rec\_coupling}) & H\,II, He\,II, He\,III & the balance coefficient is $\alpha_B+y\,\alpha_1$, $y$ the fraction of the ground-capture photons the recombined species does not re-absorb in the cell (case~A in a thin cell, case~B in a thick one); \texttt{False} $=$ case~B \\
Recombination radiation absorbed on the spot (def.\ on, \texttt{He\_rec\_coupling}) & He\,II\,$\to$\,He\,I, He\,III\,$\to$\,He\,II & He\,II: the case-B captures leave through their own exits (the 584\,\AA\ line, the 19.8\,eV $2\,^3$S line, the $2\,^1$S two-photon continuum of Drake, Victor \& Dalgarno 1969), shared by the network's rates as in Draine (2011, sect.~14.3.2, 15.5) and Osterbrock \& Ferland (2006, sect.~2.4); He\,III: the ground capture, the direct capture into $n=2$ (Seaton 1959), He\,II Ly$\alpha$ and the $2s$ two-photon continuum (Nussbaumer \& Schmutz 1984; $A(2s)=526.823$\,s$^{-1}$ of Drake 1986), with the $2s$ share of Pengelly (1964) and the $2s$--$2p$ ion mixing of Pengelly \& Seaton (1964). Every photon is shared among H\,I, He\,I, He\,II, H$_2$ and the metal ions by their opacity in the cell; what leaves the cell is dropped \\
He\,I 584\,\AA\ resonance line & He\,I $2\,^1$P\,$\to$\,$1\,^1$S & scattered by He\,I in the cell and converted into the $2\,^1$S two-photon continuum with probability $1/912.0$ per scattering (NIST $A$ values), a flight ending in continuum absorption, line scattering or escape, after Wood, Mathis \& Ercolano (2004, sect.~4.1); in a cell thick in line and continuum the continuum share tends to their eq.~(17) with every continuum absorber in place of H\,I. Doppler mean cross section, no frequency redistribution, and no electron-collisional $2\,^1$P\,$\to$\,$2\,^1$S transfer (validity stated at the code) \\
Metastable & He\,II\,$\to2\,^3$S, He\,II\,$\to$\,excited singlets & case~B split into triplet and singlet captures with the triplet share of Hummer \& Storey (1998) Table~5 (0.771 at $10^4$\,K), so ground $+$ singlets $+$ triplets $=$ the Badnell $\alpha_A$ \\
Electron collisions & He\,$1\,^1$S\,$\leftrightarrow2\,^3$S, $2\,^3$S\,$\to2\,^1$S, $2\,^1$P & $8.629132\times10^{-6}\,\Upsilon\,e^{-E/kT}/(g\sqrt{T})$ with the Bray et al.\ (2000) effective collision strengths (their table inside $10^{3.75}$--$10^{5.75}$\,K, the CHIANTI~11.0.2 he\_1 temperature dependence joined continuously outside it) and NIST level energies; the $2\,^3$S$\to1\,^1$S de-excitation $q_{31g}$ is the detailed-balance reverse of the excitation \\
 & He\,$1\,^1$S\,$\to n\,^3L$, $2\,^3$S\,$\to n\,^1L$ ($n\ge3$) & the excitations into the higher triplets cascade into $2\,^3$S and are added to $q_{13}$ (0.019, 0.107, 0.331 of it at $5\times10^3$, $10^4$, $2\times10^4$\,K); the excitations of $2\,^3$S into the singlets above $2\,^1$P destroy it and are added to $q_{31b}$; both are coronal sums over the Bray et al.\ (2000) terms $n\le5$ \\
Penning ionization & He\,$2\,^3$S\,$+$\,H & Garc\'{i}a Mu\~{n}oz (2025) Maxwell average of the Movre \& Meyer (1997) cross sections (always on); the total quenches the metastable, 0.9 of it is Penning (He\,$+$\,H$^+$\,$+\,e^-$) and 0.1 associative (HeH$^+$\,$+\,e^-$) \\
 & He\,$2\,^3$S\,$+$\,H$_2$ & Garc\'{i}a Mu\~{n}oz (2025) network rows (Cohen \& Lane 1977 cross sections; 500--10\,000\,K), split 0.9 Penning (H$_2^+$) : 0.1 associative (H\,$+$\,HeH$^+$). Active only when a molecular run also tracks the triplet \\
RR + DR & C, N, O, Mg, Si, Na, K, S, Ca\,II (+ stages) & Badnell (2006, ApJS 167, 334) RR + Badnell adf48 DR (TAMOC clist\_K) \\
RR only & Ca\,I (Ca\,II$+e$) & Shull \& Van Steenberg (1982); no DR \\
RR + DR & Fe\,I, Fe\,II & Huang et al.\ (2023) analytic fit; the Badnell DR project does not cover these isoelectronic sequences (see the note below the table) \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Charge exchange (charge transfer)}}\\[2pt]
metal\,$+$\,H/H$^+$ (default) & group~A: the 23 rows of Huang et al.\ (2023, ApJ 951, 123) Table~4, 19 carried & the reaction list of that table, each rate from the original source where it could be read (Kingdon \& Ferland 1996 and the calculations behind it: Allan et al.\ 1988, Butler \& Dalgarno 1979, 1980, Neufeld \& Dalgarno 1987, Gargaud et al.\ 1982; Kimura et al.\ 1996; Stancil et al.\ 1998, 1999; Zhao et al.\ 2005; Rutherford \& Vroom 1972; Dutta et al.\ 2001; Watanabe et al.\ 2002). The direction the source calculated or measured is carried as published; the other is computed from it by detailed balance with NIST internal partition functions. Four reverses (Mg$^+$, Fe$^+$, Si$^+$, Na$^+$\,$+$\,H) are not carried, their forward ending in an excited or radiative product \\
density-dependent reverses & N\,$+$\,H$^+$, S$^+$\,$+$\,H, C\,$+$\,S$^+$ & for the three forwards whose sources name their product levels, the reverse is the thermal one times the product populations of a five-level statistical equilibrium of N\,I, S\,II and C\,I at the cell's $(T,n_e)$ (CHIANTI~11.0.2 $A$ values and electron collision strengths); it equals the thermal reverse at the collisional limit \\
He\,$+$\,H/H$^+$ (default on, \texttt{He\_H\_charge\_}\allowbreak\texttt{exchange}) & group~B and He$^{2+}$\,$+$\,H & He$^+$\,$+$\,H: radiative (Stancil, Lepp \& Dalgarno 1998, Table~1 row~19, $1.20\times10^{-15}(T/300)^{0.25}$, from Zygelman et al.\ 1989) $+$ non-radiative (Loreau, Ryabchenko \& Vaeck 2014, by reciprocity, with $\sigma_r\propto\sqrt{(x-x_{\rm th})/(15-x_{\rm th})}$ between the threshold $x_{\rm th}=13.655$ and 15\,eV/u where their calculation starts); He\,$+$\,H$^+$: detailed balance of the non-radiative channel; He$^{2+}$\,$+$\,H\,$\to$\,He$^+$\,$+$\,H$^+$\,$+h\nu$: West, Lane \& Cohen (1982), Maxwellian average of their cross section, no reverse. Applied by dedicated routines in every helium-bearing system, \emph{not} gated by \texttt{cx\_full} \\
full network (\texttt{cx\_full}) & groups~C, D: metal$+$He/He$^+$, metal$+$metal & Table~4 reaction list with the originals where read (Kimura et al.\ 1993; Zhao et al.\ 2004; Satta et al.\ 2013; Prasad \& Huntress 1980; Rutherford \& Vroom 1972; Chenel et al.\ 2010; Kwolek et al.\ 2019; Lavvas et al.\ 2014), reverses by detailed balance; C$^+$, O$^+$, Si$^+$\,$+$\,He and Fe$^+$\,$+$\,O, Fe$^+$\,$+$\,N, Na$^+$\,$+$\,Ca are not carried; C\,$+$\,Si$^+$ as printed \\
O$^{2+}$, N$^{2+}$\,$+$\,H (default on, \texttt{cx\_O2p\_H}, \texttt{cx\_N2p\_H}) & group~E, not in Table~4 & Barrag\'an et al.\ (2006) Tables~1 and 2, interpolated over $10^2$--$10^5$\,K; no reverse; not gated by \texttt{cx\_full} \\
heat (channel \texttt{heat\_charge\_}\allowbreak\texttt{exchange}) & every applied reaction & the energy defect less the excitation of a product state that leaves as radiation (product states from the sources read; Fe$^+$ products in the near-resonant z$^4$F/z$^4$D terms) \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Radiative cooling}}\\[2pt]
recombination & H\,II, He\,II, He\,III, metal ions & the kinetic energy of the captured electrons, $kT\alpha\,(3/2+{\rm d}\ln\alpha/{\rm d}\ln T)$, with the coefficient $\alpha$ the ionization balance uses (for H\,II, He\,II and He\,III the net coefficient $\alpha_B+y\,\alpha_1$, at the frozen escape weight; for the metals their RR$+$DR coefficient); checked against Hummer (1994) \\
coll.\ excitation & H\,I & coronal sum over the CHIANTI~11.0.2 h\_1 levels (Anderson et al.\ 2000 collision strengths), observed energies, the $1s\to2s,2p$ rates the H($n{=}2$) model uses \\
 & He\,I & coronal sum over the 28 terms $n\le5$ of Bray et al.\ (2000); with the He\,$2\,^3$S tracked, the $1\,^1$S$\to2\,^3$S channel is charged with the network's own excitation and its detailed-balance reverse (a net that turns negative where the metastable is overpopulated), plus the triplet cascade cooling of $2\,^3$S (Bray terms $n\le5$) and the thresholds of its excitations into the singlets \\
 & He\,II & coronal sum over the CHIANTI~11.0.2 he\_2 levels (Ballance 2003) \\
coll.\ ionization, free--free & H, He, metals & the measured ionization potential per event (He\,$2\,^3$S and the metal ions included); free--free bremsstrahlung with the van Hoof et al.\ (2014) thermal Gaunt factor at $\gamma^2=Z^2\,{\rm Ry}/kT$, Ry the infinite-mass Rydberg (their eq.~21 and sect.~2; ${\rm Ry}/k=157887.5$\,K), weighted by ion net charge \\
CHIANTI descaling & every CHIANTI-based coefficient & the Burgess--Tully scaled collision strengths descaled with the natural cubic spline of CHIANTI's \texttt{descale\_scups.pro}, as CHIANTI prescribes \\
H$_3^+$ infrared (molecular runs) & H$_3^+$ & Miller et al.\ (2013) LTE emission per molecule (Table~5 $z(T)$ fits) times their Table~6 non-LTE departure factor $s(T,n_{\rm H_2})$, interpolated as a power law in $n({\rm H_2})$ between the columns and linearly in $T$; optically thin. The dominant coolant of a molecular base, where every atomic channel above is exponentially suppressed \\
metal line cooling & C\,I/II, N\,I/II, O\,I/II & the ground term in statistical equilibrium (row below) plus the channels that leave it from $(T,n_e)$ statistical-equilibrium tables of CHIANTI~11.0.2 (default); legacy AIOLOS fits via \texttt{cno\_cool 0} (no N cooling) \\
 & Mg\,II & closed-form fit (0.2\%) to the coronal sum over the 32 bound levels of the CHIANTI~11.0.2 model with the observed level energies \\
 & Mg\,I, Ca\,II & $(T,n_e)$ statistical-equilibrium tables of CHIANTI~11.0.2 carrying every channel of the ion \\
 & Na\,I\,D & Van Regemorter with effective $\bar g=0.2$ (not in CHIANTI) \\
 & Fe\,I & NIST $f$-values $+$ Van Regemorter, lower levels Boltzmann-populated: the high-density (LTE) limit, an upper limit at most 2.0 times the low-density value \\
 & Fe\,II & $(T,n_e)$ multilevel statistical-equilibrium table (CHIANTI) \\
 & all $(T,n_e)$ tables & above their top density the last slope in $\log n_e$ is carried on, clipped to $[-1,0]$ \\
ground-term fine structure (statistical equilibrium) & C\,I $2p^2\,^3P$, C\,II $2p\,^2P$, N\,II $2p^2\,^3P$, O\,I $2p^4\,^3P$ & the split ground term is solved in exact statistical equilibrium at the local $(n_e, n_{\rm H\,I})$ instead of the low-density coronal limit, which overstates these lines by 2.9--8.3 decades at the base. Electron collision strengths from CHIANTI v11.0.2; H-atom rates from Yan \& Babb (2023) for C\,I/N\,II, Abrahamsson, Krems \& Dalgarno (2007) for O\,I, Barinovs et al.\ (2005) for C\,II. The complete set: the other coolants have a single ground level, are built from permitted lines (Fe\,I), or already use a statistical-equilibrium table (Fe\,II) \\
line trapping & the eight ground-term fine-structure lines & line-center escape probability from the column above the cell, Hollenbach \& McKee (1979) / de Jong, Boland \& Dalgarno (1980) plane-parallel Doppler form renormalized to $\beta(0)=1$; applied as $A_{ul}\!\to\!\beta A_{ul}$ \emph{inside} the statistical-equilibrium solution, the only placement correct in both the low- and high-density limits. Other lines, including Mg\,II h\&k, are treated as optically thin \\
validity floor of the coronal fits & all CHIANTI-derived coefficients & the coronal part is cut off smoothly below $10^{3}$\,K (the lower end of the fitted range), continuous in value and $dT$-slope and bit-identical above it; only the explicitly solved ground-term fine-structure terms survive below. The roll-off is $\exp(-x^2)$ with $x=(T_{\rm floor}/T-1)/w$ and $w=0.1$ by default, settable as \texttt{Coronal cutoff width:}. With the C/N/O ground-term fine-structure floors saturated no coronal cooling survives below the floor for the guard to remove, and the base-cell balance temperature of all four paper planets is identical over $w = 0.02$--$1.2$. Not applied to the legacy AIOLOS branch (\texttt{cno\_cool 0}) \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Photoelectric heating}}\\[2pt]
secondary ionization (def.\ on, \texttt{Secondary\_}\allowbreak\texttt{ionization}) & H\,I, He\,I, H$_2$ & Shull \& van Steenberg (1985) $f_{\rm ion}(x)$ fits with the Dalgarno, Yan \& Liu (1999) energy dependence, and Dalgarno et al.'s heating fraction $f_{\rm heat}(x,E_0)$, H$_2$ target channel and molecular heat returns; photoelectrons with $E_0>30\,$eV drive secondary H\,I / He\,I / H$_2$ ionizations, heating scaled to $f_{\rm heat}$; the He\,$2^3$S photoelectron and the metal photoelectrons and Auger electrons feed the same heating and secondary-source budget, and so do the photoelectrons of the recombination radiation absorbed on the spot \\
photoelectrons of 10.2--30\,eV (opt-in, \texttt{Low-energy}\allowbreak\texttt{ electron partition}) & H\,I, He\,I & Furlanetto \& Stoever (2010) Monte Carlo tables of $f_{\rm heat}$ and of the H\,I and He\,I ionizations per primary, primordial He/H; the H\,I line photons leave the cell; stellar and recombination photoelectrons; cells whose solved state holds no H$_2$ only; default: deposited whole as heat \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Excited hydrogen H($n{=}2$) and Ly$\alpha$}}\\[2pt]
$2s/2p$ rate equilibrium & H($n{=}2$) & the level balance of Christie, Arras \& Li (2013, eqs.~12--13); $A(2p\to1s)=6.2649\times10^8$\,s$^{-1}$ (NIST), $A(2s\to1s)=8.22461$\,s$^{-1}$ (Drake 1986, eq.~27); $1s\leftrightarrow2s,2p$ collisions from the CHIANTI~11.0.2 h\_1 strengths and their detailed-balance reverses; $2s$--$2p$ mixing by electrons, Seaton (1955) eq.~(55), and by H$^+$, He$^+$ and He$^{2+}$, Pengelly \& Seaton (1964) with their cut-off; in a molecular run the H$_2^+$ and HeH$^+$ dissociative recombinations as an $n{=}2$ source \\
recombination cascade to $n{=}2$ & H & the case-B coefficient of the balance, split with the $2s$ fraction of Pengelly (1964, Table~I) \\
Balmer continuum & H($n{=}2$) & $\sigma_2=1.4\times10^{-17}$\,cm$^2$ at the $n=2$ edge (Seaton 1959, $1.378\times10^{-17}$ with his Gaunt factor), falling as $E^{-3}$, over the run's own spectrum from $e_{\rm th,HI}/4$ to $e_{\rm th,HI}$ \\
Ly$\alpha$ pumping field (mode~0) & H & Huang et al.\ (2017) Eq.~6 \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\textbf{Transmission line data (\texttt{EXHALE\_transit.py})}}\\[2pt]
$\lambda$, $f$-value, $A_{ki}$ & He\,10830, Ly$\alpha$, H$\alpha$, H$\beta$, Mg\,II, Ca\,II\,H\&K, Na\,I\,D, O\,I\,1302/1304/1306 & NIST ASD \\
line profile & all & Voigt (Faddeeva, \texttt{scipy.special.wofz}) \\
\end{longtable}

\noindent
\textbf{Note on Fe.} Iron recombination uses the Huang et al.\ (2023) analytic
RR+DR fit while every other metal uses the Badnell tabulation, and this is not a
deferred item: no Badnell fit exists for either stage. Fe\,I is formed by
recombining Fe\,II (Mn-like, 25 electrons) and Fe\,II by Fe\,III (Cr-like, 24),
whereas the Badnell dielectronic-recombination project reaches the phosphorus
isoelectronic sequence (15 electrons) as of its paper~XVI (2022). Ca\,I has the
same problem one sequence lower (K-like, 19 electrons) and likewise keeps a
non-Badnell rate. \textbf{Note on completeness.} The metal grid carries the stages
needed for the XUV-driven ionization balance and the observed NUV/optical lines:
three stages of C, N, O, Mg, Si, Ca, Fe and two of Na, K, S. Twelve of the 27
carry a line-cooling channel (C\,I, C\,II, N\,I, N\,II, O\,I, O\,II, Mg\,I,
Mg\,II, Ca\,II, Na\,I, Fe\,I, Fe\,II); the others (the doubly ionized stages,
Si\,I, Si\,II, Ca\,I, Na\,II, K\,I, K\,II, S\,I, S\,II) cool only through
recombination, collisional ionization and free--free emission, where each
applies. Higher stages
and additional elements are not modeled.
